1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
11	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
12	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
13	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
14	One-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.
15	
16	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
17	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
18	The 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.
19	The gap between $\Kempirical$ and $\Ktheory$ remains open; the neural probe result (Qwen 2v3 $\auc = 0.6159$, marginally above $\theta$) tentatively suggests the bound may be near-tight, though a single frozen-encoder probe is insufficient to establish this.
20	A DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe confirms this pattern: $\Kempirical^{\text{DeBERTa}} = \Kempirical^{\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code, but $\Kempirical^{\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at exactly one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).
21	Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\rho > 0.90$); $\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
22	ANOVA confirms this layered pattern: Scorer explains modest variance ($\eta^2_p{=}.166$) with a significant Generator$\times$Scorer interaction ($\eta^2_p{=}.146$; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}).
23	Self-scoring inflates pairwise AUC: OLMo shows the largest gap (1v2 $\Delta$ = +11.5\,pp; self-scored $\Kempirical$=2 vs.\ cross-scored $\Kempirical$=1), followed by Pythia (+4.7\,pp) and Mistral-7B (+2.4\,pp).
24	Cross-scoring provides a conservative, deployment-realistic estimate of $K^*$, as real-world contamination originates from unknown generators.
25	
26	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
27	Web text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4).
28	This elevation is not driven by \LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\Delta\auc = {-}0.021$).
29	Code (Python) provides an informative contrast: despite rigid syntactic structure, it yields $\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the arXiv elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity per se.
30	Corpus curators should calibrate depth-aware filtering to source characteristics rather than assuming a universal boundary.
31	
32	\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}
33	Retrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (3v4 $\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.
34	Prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.
35	
36	\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}
37	$\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations cluster at 1v2 $\auc$ 0.500--0.598, well separated from the $\Kempirical{=}2$ range (0.611--0.696); $\Kempirical{=}1$ is a text property (these models produce first-generation output so human-like that statistical features cannot distinguish depth~1 from depth~2), not a classifier failure.
38	Larger models reduce 0v1 JSD (0.077 at 8B vs.\ 0.15--0.27 at $\leq$1.5B), suggesting 13B+ may increasingly yield $\Kempirical{=}1$.
39	
40	\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}
41	Greedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$), with the borderline 1v2 score (2 of 5 folds above $\theta$) illustrating that the boundary is continuous rather than a cliff (Table~\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}; Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
42	
43	\paragraph{What $\Kempirical$ reveals.}
44	$\Kempirical$ establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.
45

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Across 10 prompt-chain configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--8B), $\Kempirical \leq 2$ for all web text sources ($\theta{=}0.60$); arXiv reaches $\Kempirical{=}3$ across three families.
5	This one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary; binary curation most effectively minimizes MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\,pp vs.\ $-$1.53\,pp), and generator-agnostic features enable deployment without retraining (80--92\% retention, $\rho > 0.90$); tracking $\Kempirical$ at larger scale remains open.
6

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LAvW2RyswVsbw2k8Zh7B55`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	This gap cuts across four lines of work: binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.
11	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
12	
13	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$.
14	We empirically measure a lower bound $\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
15	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \geq 1$ where the pairwise $\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
16	
17	Our contributions:
18	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
19	  \item \textbf{Measuring the discrimination boundary}: $\Kempirical \leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations (6 architecture families at 1B--8B scale) and 4 decoding strategies; $\Kempirical{=}2$ for 6/10 spanning three families ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
20	  A single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content; this finding establishes binary sufficiency by measurement, reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and scale.
21	  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
22	  \item \textbf{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) and code yield $\Kempirical{\leq}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
23	  \item \textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining; $\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
24	\end{enumerate}
25	
26	\noindent As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering for web-text filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\ base $0.6018$; Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
27	
```

  "toolUseResult": {

    "file": {
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
      "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThis gap cuts across four lines of work: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure a lower bound $\\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{Measuring the discrimination boundary}: $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations (6 architecture families at 1B--8B scale) and 4 decoding strategies; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for 6/10 spanning three families ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  A single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content; this finding establishes binary sufficiency by measurement, reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and scale.\n  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) and code yield $\\Kempirical{\\leq}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering for web-text filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$; Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",
      "numLines": 27,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 27
    }
  },

28	The DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).
29	Here $\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point motivated by Cohen's small-effect-size convention ($h \approx 0.20$\footnote{Cohen's $h = 2\arcsin\!\sqrt{0.60} - 2\arcsin\!\sqrt{0.50} \approx 0.2014$ (small effect for proportions); the equivalent $d = \sqrt{2}\,\Phi^{-1}\!(0.60) \approx 0.36$ places $\theta$ in the small-to-medium range on Cohen's $d$ scale.}; justified below).
30	All pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\alpha_{\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
31	
32	\paragraph{Threshold justification.}
33	$\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by three independent lines of evidence.
34	First, Cohen's $h \approx 0.20$, a conventional small-effect-size boundary.
35	Second, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).
36	Third, the conclusion $\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp discontinuity; only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift ($\theta{=}0.55$: $\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\theta{=}0.70$: $\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).
37	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.70]$, enabling practitioners to select $\theta$ according to their false-positive tolerance.
38	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
39

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}
5	Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong, fu2025theoretical}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, wang2025unveiling}.
6	Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.
7	Markov chain convergence theory predicts that recursive generation converges to a stationary distribution~\cite{fu2025theoretical}, complementing the DPI's mutual information monotonicity; empirically, detection accuracy erodes with each generation~\cite{hao2025rewrite}, consistent with our measured discrimination boundary.
8	This literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.
9	
10	\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
11	Binary detectors~\cite{mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, hans2024binoculars, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate; other systems such as Ghostbuster~\cite{verma2024ghostbuster} and GPTZero~\cite{liang2023gpt}, as well as benchmarks like RAID~\cite{dugan2024raid}, likewise target the binary task without estimating generational depth.
12	Recent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\cite{hao2025rewrite}; CoDeC~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses contamination via membership inference.
13	None estimates recursive generation depth.
14	
15	\paragraph{Data selection and curation.}
16	DSIR~\cite{xie2023dsir}, D4~\cite{tirumala2023d4}, and DoReMi~\cite{xie2023doremi} are depth-agnostic; Drayson et al.~\cite{drayson2025} combined binary detection with importance resampling, a special case when $\Kstar = 1$.
17	
18	\paragraph{Ordinal classification and probing.}
19	Our OBD classifier follows Frank \& Hall's~\cite{frank2001ordinal} binary decomposition; our neural probe extends probing~\cite{alain2017understanding, belinkov2022probing} to the discrimination boundary.
20	
```

    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{colortbl}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage[section]{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable).\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 4 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text configurations across six architecture families at 1B--8B scale ($\\theta{=}0.60$); structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families.\nA single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect that concentrates all exploitable signal at the human-vs.-machine boundary.\nGenerator-agnostic statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has four main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv, Code), and prompt-chain generation; whether $\\Kempirical{=}2$ persists at larger scales (e.g., 13B+) remains untested, and retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\nOur model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.\n(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\clearpage\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 10 prompt-chain configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--8B), $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text sources ($\\theta{=}0.60$); arXiv reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three families.\nThis one-wash laundering effect concentrates all exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary; binary curation most effectively minimizes MMLU degradation ($-$0.77\\,pp vs.\\ $-$1.53\\,pp), and generator-agnostic features enable deployment without retraining (80--92\\% retention, $\\rho > 0.90$); tracking $\\Kempirical$ at larger scale remains open.\n",

    "newString": "\\noindent As a consistency check under extreme (100\\%) contamination, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering: binary curation minimizes MMLU degradation ($0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$); at realistic ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all strategies converge (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThis gap cuts across four lines of work: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure a lower bound $\\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{Measuring the discrimination boundary}: $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations (6 architecture families at 1B--8B scale) and 4 decoding strategies; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for 6/10 spanning three families ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  A single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content; this finding establishes binary sufficiency by measurement, reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and scale.\n  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) and code yield $\\Kempirical{\\leq}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering for web-text filtering: binary curation minimizes degradation most effectively (MMLU $0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$; Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/experiments_c3.tex",
    "oldString": "At realistic contamination ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all filtering strategies converge ($p > 0.12$), consistent with the one-wash laundering finding that depth-aware curation offers marginal benefit when $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nFor $\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations, depth-aware curation reduces to standard binary filtering, consistent with $\\Kempirical{=}1$ implying no exploitable depth signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.",
    "newString": "At realistic contamination ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all filtering strategies converge ($p > 0.12$), consistent with the one-wash laundering finding that depth-aware curation offers marginal benefit when $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nThis extreme setting serves to validate $\\Kempirical$'s predictive power: even though effect sizes vanish at realistic contamination ratios, the predicted strategy ordering holds, confirming that $\\Kempirical$ captures meaningful distributional structure.\nFor $\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations, depth-aware curation reduces to standard binary filtering, consistent with $\\Kempirical{=}1$ implying no exploitable depth signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Consistency Check: $\\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}\n\\label{sec:filtering}\n\n$\\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.\nWe present this as a consistency check under extreme (100\\%) contamination, not as an independent contribution; at realistic rates ($\\leq$10\\%), the filtering effect diminishes to $\\Delta \\leq 0.1$\\,pp (not significant; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nWe compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\\textbf{graduated} ($w = \\exp(-\\alpha \\cdot \\hat{d})$, $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1,\\ldots,0.9\\}$), (v)~\\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\\textbf{DSIR}~\\citep{xie2023dsir}.\nWe fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via LoRA (rank~8, $\\alpha_{\\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (this 100\\% synthetic setting is an upper bound~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \\exp(-\\alpha \\hat{d})$. Mean $\\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\\alpha$ sweep in Figure~\\ref{fig:filtering_strategies}.}\n\\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}\n\\end{table}\n\nBinary-ours minimizes MMLU degradation most effectively (Table~\\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}), reducing the gap from $-$1.53\\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\\,pp (${\\sim}$50\\% reduction; $p_{\\text{corr}} < 0.05$ vs.\\ all alternatives).\nThese effects are measured in a 100\\% synthetic upper-bound setting; real-world contamination ratios are typically lower, and effect sizes would scale accordingly.\nThe strategy ordering is interpretable through $\\Kempirical$: binary filtering exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies assign weights to depths 2--5 based on near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise.\nGraduated filtering converges monotonically toward binary: at $\\alpha{=}0.9$, it matches binary-ours on HellaSwag exactly ($\\Delta = 0$, $p_{\\text{corr}} = 1.00$) but remains significantly worse on MMLU ($p_{\\text{corr}} = 0.011$).\nDSIR is indistinguishable from no-filter, confirming that depth-aware features are necessary for curation in recursively contaminated corpora (Figure~\\ref{fig:filtering_strategies} in Appendix).\nAt realistic contamination ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all filtering strategies converge ($p > 0.12$), consistent with the one-wash laundering finding that depth-aware curation offers marginal benefit when $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nFor $\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations, depth-aware curation reduces to standard binary filtering, consistent with $\\Kempirical{=}1$ implying no exploitable depth signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.\n",

          " DSIR is indistinguishable from no-filter, confirming that depth-aware features are necessary for curation in recursively contaminated corpora (Figure~\\ref{fig:filtering_strategies} in Appendix).",
          " At realistic contamination ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all filtering strategies converge ($p > 0.12$), consistent with the one-wash laundering finding that depth-aware curation offers marginal benefit when $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).",
          "+This extreme setting serves to validate $\\Kempirical$'s predictive power: even though effect sizes vanish at realistic contamination ratios, the predicted strategy ordering holds, confirming that $\\Kempirical$ captures meaningful distributional structure.",
          " For $\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations, depth-aware curation reduces to standard binary filtering, consistent with $\\Kempirical{=}1$ implying no exploitable depth signal beyond the 0v1 boundary."

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; the neural probe result (Qwen 2v3 $\\auc = 0.6159$, marginally above $\\theta$) tentatively suggests the bound may be near-tight, though a single frozen-encoder probe is insufficient to establish this.\nA DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe confirms this pattern: $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code, but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at exactly one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$); $\\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nANOVA confirms this layered pattern: Scorer explains modest variance ($\\eta^2_p{=}.166$) with a significant Generator$\\times$Scorer interaction ($\\eta^2_p{=}.146$; Table~\\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}).\nSelf-scoring inflates pairwise AUC: OLMo shows the largest gap (1v2 $\\Delta$ = +11.5\\,pp; self-scored $\\Kempirical$=2 vs.\\ cross-scored $\\Kempirical$=1), followed by Pythia (+4.7\\,pp) and Mistral-7B (+2.4\\,pp).\nCross-scoring provides a conservative, deployment-realistic estimate of $K^*$, as real-world contamination originates from unknown generators.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$).\nCode (Python) provides an informative contrast: despite rigid syntactic structure, it yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the arXiv elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity per se.\nCorpus curators should calibrate depth-aware filtering to source characteristics rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}\n$\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations cluster at 1v2 $\\auc$ 0.500--0.598, well separated from the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ range (0.611--0.696); $\\Kempirical{=}1$ is a text property (these models produce first-generation output so human-like that statistical features cannot distinguish depth~1 from depth~2), not a classifier failure.\nLarger models reduce 0v1 JSD (0.077 at 8B vs.\\ 0.15--0.27 at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting 13B+ may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$), with the borderline 1v2 score (2 of 5 folds above $\\theta$) illustrating that the boundary is continuous rather than a cliff (Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\n\n\\paragraph{What $\\Kempirical$ reveals.}\n$\\Kempirical$ establishes why binary detection suffices for web text (depth${>}1$ is invisible to statistical features), identifies domains where finer strategies are viable (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies how scale, scorer, and threshold jointly shape the boundary.\n",

    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{colortbl}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage[section]{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable).\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 4 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text configurations across six architecture families at 1B--8B scale ($\\theta{=}0.60$); structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families.\nA single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect that concentrates all exploitable signal at the human-vs.-machine boundary.\nGenerator-agnostic statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nUnder extreme contamination (100\\% synthetic), $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives; at realistic ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all strategies converge ($p > 0.12$).\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has four main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv, Code), and prompt-chain generation; whether $\\Kempirical{=}2$ persists at larger scales (e.g., 13B+) remains untested, and retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\nOur model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.\n(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\clearpage\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

    "newString": "\\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer} (within continuation mode): 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown continuation-mode generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThis gap cuts across four lines of work: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}); membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question; the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool; and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure a lower bound $\\Kempirical$ on the discrimination boundary (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistical features and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{Measuring the discrimination boundary}: $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations (6 architecture families at 1B--8B scale) and 4 decoding strategies; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for 6/10 spanning three families ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  A single generation pass renders web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content; this finding establishes binary sufficiency by measurement, reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and scale.\n  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) and code yield $\\Kempirical{\\leq}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent As a consistency check under extreme (100\\%) contamination, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering: binary curation minimizes MMLU degradation ($0.5941$ vs.\\ base $0.6018$); at realistic ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all strategies converge (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n",

          "-  \\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer}: 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).",
          "+  \\item \\textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer} (within continuation mode): 15 statistical features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown continuation-mode generators without retraining; $\\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).",

    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{colortbl}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage[section]{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable).\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 4 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text configurations across six architecture families at 1B--8B scale ($\\theta{=}0.60$); structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families.\nA single generation pass suffices to render general web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect that concentrates all exploitable signal at the human-vs.-machine boundary.\nAcross continuation-mode generators, generator-agnostic statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nUnder extreme contamination (100\\% synthetic), $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives; at realistic ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), all strategies converge ($p > 0.12$).\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has four main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv, Code), and prompt-chain generation; whether $\\Kempirical{=}2$ persists at larger scales (e.g., 13B+) remains untested, and retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\nOur model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.\n(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\clearpage\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

  "old_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ across all 10 configurations: 6/10 yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Qwen$\\times$4, LLaMA-8B, Pythia-1.4B; 1v2 $\\auc$: 0.607--0.696; $p < 0.001$), while OLMo-1B, Mistral-7B, Pythia-6.9B, and Gemma-2-2B yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.500--0.598; Section~\\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).\nUnder Bonferroni correction (99.5\\% CIs), three of six $\\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations retain significance; the three marginal cases (Pythia-1.4B, Qwen-cont, LLaMA-8B; CI lower bounds 0.593--0.599) reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure.\nBy architecture family: Qwen (4/4) and LLaMA (1/1) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}2$ at nominal significance; OLMo (1/1), Mistral (1/1), and Gemma (1/1) yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$; Pythia exhibits a scale-dependent split (1.4B: $\\Kempirical{=}2$, 6.9B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).",
  "new_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ across all 10 configurations: 6/10 yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.607--0.696; $p < 0.001$), while 4/10 yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.500--0.598; Section~\\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).\nAcross six architecture families, three (Qwen, Pythia, LLaMA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}2$ in at least one configuration; the remaining three (OLMo, Gemma-2, Mistral) yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nPythia exhibits a scale-dependent split (1.4B: $\\Kempirical{=}2$, 6.9B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); Qwen dominates the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ count (4 of 6 configurations).\nUnder Bonferroni correction (99.5\\% CIs), three of six $\\Kempirical{=}2$ configurations retain significance; the three marginal cases (Pythia-1.4B, Qwen-cont, LLaMA-8B; CI lower bounds 0.593--0.599) reflect narrow effect sizes rather than classification failure."

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "Here $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point motivated by Cohen's small-effect-size convention ($h \\approx 0.20$\\footnote{Cohen's $h = 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.60} - 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.50} \\approx 0.2014$ (small effect for proportions); the equivalent $d = \\sqrt{2}\\,\\Phi^{-1}\\!(0.60) \\approx 0.36$ places $\\theta$ in the small-to-medium range on Cohen's $d$ scale.}; justified below).",
    "newString": "Here $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction. (Simplified notation; see Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar} for the formal $\\Kempirical$ definition.)}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC may exhibit non-monotonicity at deeper depths due to classifier and feature interactions (e.g., Pythia-1.4B greedy 4v5 $\\auc = 1.0$ vs.\\ 3v4 $= 0.522$; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point motivated by Cohen's small-effect-size convention ($h \\approx 0.20$\\footnote{Cohen's $h = 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.60} - 2\\arcsin\\!\\sqrt{0.50} \\approx 0.2014$ (small effect for proportions); the equivalent $d = \\sqrt{2}\\,\\Phi^{-1}\\!(0.60) \\approx 0.36$ places $\\theta$ in the small-to-medium range on Cohen's $d$ scale.}; justified below).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by three independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, Cohen's $h \\approx 0.20$, a conventional small-effect-size boundary.\nSecond, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nThird, the conclusion $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$, enabling practitioners to select $\\theta$ according to their false-positive tolerance.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

          " All pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% CIs (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Threshold justification.}",
          "-$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by three independent lines of evidence.",
          "-First, Cohen's $h \\approx 0.20$, a conventional small-effect-size boundary.",
          "-Second, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).",
          "-Third, the conclusion $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).",
          "-Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$, enabling practitioners to select $\\theta$ according to their false-positive tolerance.",
          "+$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.",
          "+First, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).",
          "+Second, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures any $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.61]$ yields the same $\\Kempirical$; only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).",
          "+Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.70]$.",
          " The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.",
          " ",
          " \\subsection{Features and Classifier}"
        ]
      }
    ],

    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Ordinal classification and probing.}\nOur OBD classifier follows Frank \\& Hall's~\\cite{frank2001ordinal} binary decomposition; our neural probe extends probing~\\cite{alain2017understanding, belinkov2022probing} to the discrimination boundary.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Ordinal classification and probing.}\nOur OBD classifier follows Frank \\& Hall's~\\cite{frank2001ordinal} binary decomposition; our neural probe extends probing~\\cite{alain2017understanding, belinkov2022probing} to the discrimination boundary.\nAn orthogonal line of work embeds provenance signals proactively via text watermarking~\\cite{kirchenbauer2023watermark}; our work addresses the complementary post-hoc scenario where the generator is not controlled.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Model collapse and recursive degradation.}\nIterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong, fu2025theoretical}, diversity contraction~\\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\\cite{schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, wang2025unveiling}.\nOur prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\\cite{kazdan2025collapse}, making $\\Kempirical = 2$ a conservative lower bound.\nMarkov chain convergence theory predicts that recursive generation converges to a stationary distribution~\\cite{fu2025theoretical}, complementing the DPI's mutual information monotonicity; empirically, detection accuracy erodes with each generation~\\cite{hao2025rewrite}, consistent with our measured discrimination boundary.\nThis literature characterizes macro dynamics but provides no per-text depth estimation, the gap we address.\n\n\\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}\nBinary detectors~\\cite{mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, hans2024binoculars, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate; other systems such as Ghostbuster~\\cite{verma2024ghostbuster} and GPTZero~\\cite{liang2023gpt}, as well as benchmarks like RAID~\\cite{dugan2024raid}, likewise target the binary task without estimating generational depth.\nRecent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\\cite{hao2025rewrite}; CoDeC~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses contamination via membership inference.\nNone estimates recursive generation depth.\n\n\\paragraph{Data selection and curation.}\nDSIR~\\cite{xie2023dsir}, D4~\\cite{tirumala2023d4}, and DoReMi~\\cite{xie2023doremi} are depth-agnostic; Drayson et al.~\\cite{drayson2025} combined binary detection with importance resampling, a special case when $\\Kstar = 1$.\n\n\\paragraph{Ordinal classification and probing.}\nOur OBD classifier follows Frank \\& Hall's~\\cite{frank2001ordinal} binary decomposition; our neural probe extends probing~\\cite{alain2017understanding, belinkov2022probing} to the discrimination boundary.\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "063e7df7-788d-4691-9963-60490d649f8a",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "3ccb6587-c572-43fd-88a9-bc04f58a5ced",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "f8811e5e-f8cf-410a-9530-767f46e3da4e",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "oldString": "A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.",
    "newString": "One-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; the neural probe result (Qwen 2v3 $\\auc = 0.6159$, marginally above $\\theta$) tentatively suggests the bound may be near-tight, though a single frozen-encoder probe is insufficient to establish this.\nA DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe confirms this pattern: $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code, but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at exactly one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$); $\\Kempirical$ agreement is scorer-independent for strong-signal configurations (e.g., Qwen-rewrite retains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under all scorers) but scorer-sensitive at marginal boundaries (Qwen-continuation drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nANOVA confirms this layered pattern: Scorer explains modest variance ($\\eta^2_p{=}.166$) with a significant Generator$\\times$Scorer interaction ($\\eta^2_p{=}.146$; Table~\\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}).\nSelf-scoring inflates pairwise AUC: OLMo shows the largest gap (1v2 $\\Delta$ = +11.5\\,pp; self-scored $\\Kempirical$=2 vs.\\ cross-scored $\\Kempirical$=1), followed by Pythia (+4.7\\,pp) and Mistral-7B (+2.4\\,pp).\nCross-scoring provides a conservative, deployment-realistic estimate of $K^*$, as real-world contamination originates from unknown generators.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across three independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$).\nCode (Python) provides an informative contrast: despite rigid syntactic structure, it yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the arXiv elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity per se.\n$\\Kempirical$ varies by domain, suggesting that any future depth-aware filtering should account for source characteristics rather than assuming a universal boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}\n$\\Kempirical{=}1$ configurations cluster at 1v2 $\\auc$ 0.500--0.598, well separated from the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ range (0.611--0.696); $\\Kempirical{=}1$ is a text property (these models produce first-generation output so human-like that statistical features cannot distinguish depth~1 from depth~2), not a classifier failure.\nLarger models reduce 0v1 JSD (0.077 at 8B vs.\\ 0.15--0.27 at $\\leq$1.5B), suggesting 13B+ may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-6.9B yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ under greedy but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$), with the borderline 1v2 score (2 of 5 folds above $\\theta$) illustrating that the boundary is continuous rather than a cliff (Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding};
